## 1. Executive directive

The preceding campaigns closed an important vertical.

At the audited baseline, a coding agent can:

- author typed semantic workspace revisions;
- publish immutable workspace-independent reusable releases;
- compose exact single-file applications;
- delete source workspaces and standalone release files;
- invoke pure typed and bounded byte-stream applications;
- create durable stateful application instances;
- apply exact events against exact revisions;
- suspend on one host command;
- publish state before host execution;
- record known success, known failure, or unknown visibility;
- reconcile an unknown activation result;
- resume through another pure transition;
- inspect bounded state and history;
- replay exact history after restart;
- and tombstone an instance without identity reuse.

Do not repeat those achievements as if they were the next objective.

The current stateful vertical is deliberately narrow.

Its application profile fixes one exact decision product with these conceptual fields:

- next state;
- byte response;
- integer command tag;
- byte target.

Its command tags are coupled to:

- validate an application;
- activate an application;
- reconcile application activation.

Its production grant is coupled to one application source directory and one activation slot.

This was an effective proof of state publication, host separation, unknown outcomes, and replay.

It is not a sufficient long-term application interface.

If retained unchanged, every future host operation would either:

- add another magic integer;
- overload the byte target;
- push application-specific interpretation into the runtime;
- duplicate a new command family beside activation;
- or force a premature general effect system.

That is the highest-leverage architectural issue now.

The second issue is operational.

Current public workflows use many short-lived processes.

The latest measured durable-controller workflow used 41 processes while opening one workspace
`Engine`.

Repeated exact application loading, validation, graph flattening, lowering, Core verification,
instance replay, and command-local startup may become material as applications grow.

A long-lived runtime could eventually amortize those costs, share derived compiled units, govern
aggregate resources, schedule independent instances, route capability adapters, and provide a place
for tiered execution.

However, the repository deliberately deleted an earlier daemon because no product consumer justified
it.

Do not recreate a daemon by instinct.

Do not add a resident service merely because future JITs and schedulers often live in services.

Do not add a JIT merely because interpreters are slower in synthetic loops.

Do not add a worker pool merely because the word `runtime` suggests concurrency.

The campaign objective is:

> Replace the activation-specific stateful boundary with one exact typed application-world and
> host-interface model, prove it with two genuinely different useful applications and adapters,
> establish one topology-neutral runtime kernel, measure whether a foreground session or resident
> supervisor earns retention, add explicit aggregate resource governance and stage telemetry, and
> create a disciplined path to compiled execution without making caches, scheduling, process
> topology, or profiles semantic authority.

This is an application-generalization, runtime-boundary, resource, performance, and simplification
campaign.

It is not a feature-accumulation campaign.

The final tree must retain one coherent architecture.

Losing prototypes must leave no format, compatibility route, dormant variant, unused identity
domain, dead command, duplicate schema owner, stale fixture, or misleading documentation.

## 2. Required campaign outcomes

The campaign has mandatory outcomes and evidence-gated outcomes.

Mandatory outcomes must be implemented unless newer checkout evidence proves that an equivalent or
stronger result already exists.

Evidence-gated outcomes must be decided through bounded prototypes and representative measurement.

### 2.1 Mandatory: generalized application worlds

Replace the activation-specific stateful application ABI with one general exact typed contract.

The retained contract must allow an application to declare:

- exact exported invocation entry points;
- exact stateful transition entry points when stateful;
- exact state, event, response, command, outcome, and decision types;
- exact imported host-interface requirements;
- exact command-to-interface routing;
- exact outcome compatibility;
- exact tests;
- exact semantic policy;
- and explicit absence of authority.

The retained contract must not require magic application-wide integer command tags.

The retained contract must not require every command target to be an application digest.

The retained contract must not encode host grants inside immutable application bytes.

The retained contract must make complete versus suspended results structurally typed.

The retained contract must permit ordinary nominal application data for expected workflow outcomes.

The retained contract must keep infrastructure corruption, authority rejection, resource exhaustion,
and unknown host visibility distinguishable.

### 2.2 Mandatory: two independent host interfaces

Retain at least two useful production host interfaces with different data and authority shapes.

One may be the existing exact application activation interface after migration.

The second must not be an alias for activation.

The leading second interface is a bounded exact immutable-blob store.

A stronger alternative may replace it only when it proves more generality without importing broad
filesystem, process, network, secret, or plugin scope.

Each interface requires:

- one exact interface identity;
- closed request and outcome types;
- one narrow grant descriptor;
- production adapter;
- deterministic fake adapter;
- exact attempt and result evidence;
- known-before-visibility failure;
- unknown-after-possible-visibility behavior where applicable;
- reconciliation or idempotent repeat rules;
- bounds;
- restart behavior;
- corruption rejection;
- and cross-interface and cross-instance denial tests.

### 2.3 Mandatory: two useful durable applications

Migrate the durable release controller to the generalized interface.

Add a second useful durable application that exercises the second production adapter.

The second application must own its workflow in lkjscript semantics.

A Rust or Python driver may construct inputs and check outcomes.

The driver must not become the real workflow controller.

Both applications must remain operable after source workspaces and standalone releases are removed.

Both must survive process restart.

Both must exercise duplicate delivery, stale base rejection, denied authority, known failure,
unknown or reconciliation where applicable, bounded inspection, and deterministic replay.

### 2.4 Mandatory: topology-neutral runtime kernel

Introduce one internal runtime-kernel boundary that owns operational orchestration without becoming
semantic authority.

The runtime kernel should be able to:

- load and validate exact applications;
- open exact instance authority;
- prepare pure transitions;
- enforce resource admission;
- dispatch exact host requests through bound adapters;
- record outcomes through the instance owner;
- provide exact bounded inspection;
- collect stage measurements;
- and use bounded derived caches.

One-shot CLI commands, foreground sessions, and any resident supervisor must call this same kernel.

Do not maintain independent semantic implementations for each topology.

### 2.5 Mandatory: stage telemetry and reproducible measurements

Instrument enough of the complete workflow to separate:

- process and runtime startup;
- application read;
- envelope decode;
- canonical re-encode;
- release graph validation;
- release tests when applicable;
- closure flattening;
- lowering;
- Core IR verification;
- execution;
- public value validation and materialization;
- instance open;
- record-chain validation;
- deterministic replay;
- transition preparation;
- state publication;
- grant validation;
- adapter preparation;
- host action;
- outcome publication;
- queue delay;
- cache lookup;
- cache population;
- cache eviction;
- and response encoding.

Do not expose unstable internal timings as semantic results.

Measurements are observations, not authority.

Keep telemetry bounded and optional for ordinary operation.

### 2.6 Mandatory: explicit resource governance

Define semantic and operational resource categories separately.

Add exact admission behavior for every retained shared runtime resource.

At minimum consider:

- input bytes;
- output bytes;
- state bytes;
- history bytes;
- event bytes;
- response bytes;
- host evidence bytes;
- execution fuel;
- execution frames;
- logical cells;
- managed visible bytes;
- retained backing bytes;
- managed objects;
- application bytes;
- release graph bytes;
- loaded application count;
- compiled-unit bytes;
- cache bytes;
- profile bytes;
- queued requests;
- active transitions;
- active host operations;
- concurrent compilations;
- open instance stores;
- open files;
- temporary publication bytes;
- and total runtime resident memory observation.

Do not claim exact resident-set enforcement if only logical accounting is available.

### 2.7 Evidence-gated: foreground runtime session

Compare one-shot operation with a foreground line-delimited runtime session.

A foreground session is the leading minimum retained topology because the caller owns its lifecycle,
it can amortize derived state, and it does not require a background service.

Retain it only if it improves complete representative workflows without duplicating semantics or
weakening failure boundaries.

### 2.8 Evidence-gated: resident runtime supervisor

Prototype a local resident supervisor only when the runtime kernel makes the comparison small and
honest.

Compare:

- one-shot process per request;
- foreground standard-input/standard-output session;
- local Unix-domain-socket supervisor;
- and no retained long-lived topology.

A resident supervisor may win when it materially improves repeated exact application operation,
multi-instance admission, aggregate resource governance, or cache reuse.

It loses when lifecycle, authentication, stale process recovery, IPC, deployment, and debugging cost
outweigh complete-workflow benefit.

If it loses, delete it completely.

If it wins, make it one coherent supported topology and document startup, shutdown, recovery,
authentication, authorization, versioning, overload, and observability.

Do not call a process boundary a sandbox.

### 2.9 Evidence-gated: compiled-unit cache

Measure repeated exact applications before retaining a compiled-unit cache.

A cache may retain verified Core IR, predecoded instructions, baseline code, or another derived unit
only when its key binds every correctness-relevant input.

A cache hit must not bypass artifact validation that remains necessary for trust.

A cache miss must remain fully correct.

Eviction must be semantically invisible.

### 2.10 Evidence-gated: bytecode, baseline compiler, or JIT

Compare the measured dominant execution term against:

- interpreter dispatch improvements;
- predecoded or compact bytecode;
- a baseline native compiler;
- an optimizing tier;
- and no new execution tier.

Do not implement all tiers.

Retain at most the smallest tier that pays for itself in representative application workloads.

The explicit-frame interpreter or another simple route remains the differential oracle.

### 2.11 Mandatory: agent-economy evaluation

Reduce at least one measured development cost without weakening correctness.

Candidate costs include:

- repeated context bytes;
- repeated source discovery;
- correction depth;
- provider input tokens;
- provider cached-input tokens;
- provider output tokens;
- provider reasoning tokens;
- provider calls;
- process count;
- engine opens;
- files opened;
- source bytes opened;
- build invocations;
- clean build time;
- incremental build time;
- and review ambiguity.

Do not infer tokens or monetary savings from bytes.

Use actual provider telemetry only when exposed.

### 2.12 Mandatory: direct cutover

Backward compatibility is absent.

When the generalized interface wins:

- bump every affected format and contract deliberately;
- reject old successful formats directly;
- delete old readers and writers;
- delete activation-specific stateful profile machinery;
- delete magic command tags;
- delete compatibility tests;
- migrate retained examples;
- delete stale documentation;
- delete obsolete prompt artifacts without a current consumer;
- and leave one active path.

## 3. Product and domain model

The term `runtime host` is provisional.

Do not make it a catch-all.

Use or improve this responsibility split:

```text
semantic workspace authority
        |
        v
immutable reusable releases
        |
        v
immutable application world
        |
        +-------------------- declared host interfaces
        |
        v
durable application instance
        |
        v
topology-neutral runtime kernel
        |
        +---- pure execution engine
        |
        +---- instance store
        |
        +---- resource admission
        |
        +---- grant resolver
        |
        +---- trusted host adapters
        |
        +---- derived caches and telemetry
        |
        v
optional foreground session or runtime supervisor
```

The names may change.

The responsibilities may not collapse without a proved simplification.

The semantic engine remains distinct from the runtime kernel.

Workspace publication remains distinct from application operation.

The execution engine remains derived.

The instance store remains durable authority.

Host interfaces describe typed intent.

Host adapters implement intent.

Grants authorize adapters.

A runtime supervisor may coordinate these objects.

It may not become an undocumented source of application meaning, durable state, or authority.

A runtime process restart must reconstruct accepted state from durable authority.

Queues, in-memory handles, caches, profiles, compiled units, and scheduler state are disposable unless
a new specification deliberately makes one durable.

Do not make an implicit current workspace, current application, current instance, current grant, or
current deployment part of semantic behavior.

Every request names exact authority.

Every cache entry names exact derived input.

Every adapter call names exact interface and grant.

Every state transition names exact base revision.

Every externally visible action has exact attempt and outcome semantics.

Every public inspection is bounded.

Every omission is explicit.
